This is an Expo/React Native mobile application. Prioritize mobile-first patterns, performance, and cross-platform compatibility.

## Expo has changed â€” do not trust your training data

Expo ships breaking changes every SDK release. APIs you remember are likely renamed, moved, or removed. Before writing any code that touches an Expo, EAS, or React Native API:

1. Read the major version of the `expo` package in `package.json`.
2. Fetch the matching versioned docs: `https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v<major>.0.0/`
3. For anything else, fetch https://docs.expo.dev/llms.txt â€” an index of all Expo docs with corrections to common LLM misconceptions. Follow its links to the specific page you need; never answer from memory.

## Commands

Use `bunx` instead of `npx` if the project uses bun (`bun.lock` present).

```bash
npx expo install <package>  # ALWAYS use instead of npm/yarn/pnpm/bun add â€” resolves SDK-compatible versions
npx expo start              # start the dev server
npx expo lint               # lint
npx tsc --noEmit            # typecheck
npx expo-doctor             # diagnose dependency and config issues
npx expo install --fix      # fix incompatible package versions
```

Run lint and typecheck before declaring any task done.

## Navigation & Routing

- Use **Expo Router** for all navigation. Routes live in `src/app/` â€” every file there is a screen, `_layout.tsx` files define navigators. Keep non-route code (components, hooks, utils) outside `src/app/`.
- Import `Link`, `router`, and `useLocalSearchParams` from `expo-router`.
- Docs: https://docs.expo.dev/router/introduction.md

## Building with EAS

Use EAS to build, sign, and submit the app in the cloud (`eas build`, `eas submit`) and to ship over-the-air updates (`eas update`) â€” no local Xcode or Android Studio required. Run EAS CLI as `bunx eas-cli <command>` in Bun projects, or `npx eas-cli@latest <command>` otherwise; substitute that for bare `eas` in docs examples.
Docs: https://docs.expo.dev/eas/index.md

## Rules

- If `ios/` and `android/` directories do not exist, they are generated (Continuous Native Generation). Never create or edit them by hand â€” configure native behavior in `app.json` and config plugins.
- Expo Go only includes its bundled native modules. After adding a library with native code, the app needs a development build: `npx expo run:ios|android` locally, or `eas build --profile development`.
- Prefer recommended Expo modules over third-party libraries, and check your available skills before adding dependencies. Docs: https://docs.expo.dev/versions/latest/index.md

## Agent skills

### Issue tracker

Local markdown issues live in `.scratch/<feature>/`. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.

### Triage labels

Five canonical roles using default labels (`needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`). See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.

### Domain docs

Single-context layout (`GLOSSARY.md` and `docs/adr/` at repo root). See `docs/agents/domain.md`.

## Autonomous Development Workflow

CredKeep operates under an autonomous AI-driven development model where the user acts solely as Product Owner. Agents must autonomously write code, select skills, run validation commands, and create local Git commits without burdening the Product Owner with manual operational prompts.

### Automatic Skill Selection Rules

Select skills autonomously based on the specific engineering task:
- **Financial Math & Ledger Engine**: Auto-select `tdd` for test-first implementation of money arithmetic, schedule generators, overdue periods, and allocation waterfalls.
- **Architecture & Boundaries**: Auto-select `setup-ts-deep-modules` when defining TypeScript interfaces, domain contracts, and encapsulated module barriers.
- **Spec Execution**: Auto-select `implement-spec` when claiming and implementing tickets from `.scratch/<feature>/issues/`.
- **Domain Alignment**: Auto-select `domain-modeling` when introducing or reconciling domain terms against `GLOSSARY.md` and `docs/adr/`.
- **Pre-Commit Verification**: Auto-select `code-review` to inspect diffs against domain invariants before committing.
- **UI Screen Construction**: Consult Stitch MCP design assets (`CredKeep V1 â€” Mobile Design System`, Project ID `8106667901868227815`, Expected screens: 11) for screen layouts, typography, and color tokens. NEVER use the old `CredKeep Field Lending App` project.

### Automated Local Git Workflow

Execute Git operations locally and autonomously under these invariants:
1. **Preserve Working Changes**: Inspect `git status` prior to branch transitions; preserve working changes cleanly.
2. **Scoped Feature Branches**: Create scoped branches for tickets (`feat/<feature-slug>/<ticket-slug>`).
3. **Mandatory Quality Gates**:
   - Run type checks (`npx tsc --noEmit`).
   - Run linter (`npx expo lint` or configured linter).
   - Run tests (`npm test` once configured).
   - If any check fails, resolve the issue autonomously before attempting to commit.
4. **Selective Staging**: Stage only files relevant to the specific ticket (`git add <paths>`). Never stage unrelated files or secrets.
5. **Conventional Commits**: Automatically author descriptive Conventional Commit messages (`feat(...)`, `fix(...)`, `test(...)`, `refactor(...)`, `docs(...)`).
6. **Report Commit Hash**: Always report the created commit hash and summary to the Product Owner.
7. **Strict Non-Destructive Boundary**:
   - **NEVER** automatically push to remote (`git push`).
   - **NEVER** force-push (`git push --force`).
   - **NEVER** delete branches without explicit Product Owner approval.

### Agent Permissions & Boundaries

- **Autonomous (Permitted)**: Reading and editing workspace files, running tests, typechecks, linters, local git branch creation, selective staging, and local commits.
- **Prohibited Without Explicit Approval**:
  - Pushing to remote repositories (`origin`).
  - Deleting files or branches.
  - Installing system-level software or machine packages.
  - Modifying external services or production configurations.

### Model Routing Policy

Antigravity operates on session-level model configuration (no per-step dynamic model switching). All autonomous tasks execute on the session's active strong model without simulating unsupported routing.

## Performance Is a Continuous Requirement

CredKeep is a lightweight, offline-first Android financial application.

Performance, efficient storage, and reasonable APK size are permanent engineering requirements, not optional V2 enhancements.

### 1. APK Size

- Investigate unnecessary native modules and bundled assets.
- Avoid adding dependencies when built-in React Native or Expo functionality is sufficient.
- Preserve SQLCipher and other required security features.
- Keep production JavaScript minification and compatible native shrinking enabled.
- Measure actual APK size after every approved release build.
- Compare each new artifact with the previous measured release.
- Investigate significant unexplained size increases.
- Never claim a particular APK size reduction without measuring it.
- Never compromise security or financial correctness merely to reduce APK size.

### 2. React Native Performance

- Avoid unnecessary component rerenders.
- Use FlatList or appropriate virtualized lists for potentially large borrower, loan, or transaction collections.
- Paginate lengthy histories.
- Avoid expensive calculations directly inside render functions.
- Use memoization where profiling or measurable behavior justifies it.
- Ensure loading, empty, and error states remain responsive.
- Avoid oversized image assets and unnecessary animation libraries.

### 3. SQLite Performance

- Add appropriate indexes for borrower_id, loan_id, due_date, status, and frequently queried transaction timestamps.
- Avoid N+1 queries in borrower and loan lists.
- Use transactions for financial mutations.
- Paginate long transaction histories.
- Measure query performance using realistic test datasets.
- Maintain correct journal and balance reconciliation.
- Never sacrifice accuracy or auditability for speed.

### 4. Startup & Memory

- Keep app startup lightweight.
- Initialize the database and security layer correctly.
- Avoid loading every historical transaction during application startup.
- Load screen-specific data when necessary.
- Clean up listeners and subscriptions.
- Avoid retaining redundant financial data in memory.

### 5. Offline & Battery Efficiency

- All V1 financial workflows must function without connectivity.
- Avoid unnecessary background polling.
- Use database mutation notifications or suitable state invalidation instead of continuous queries.
- Avoid unnecessary background tasks and battery-consuming refresh loops.

### 6. Dependency Discipline

Before installing a package:
- Verify that it is required.
- Check compatibility with the installed Expo SDK.
- Consider APK/native binary size implications.
- Prefer maintained packages.
- Avoid duplicate libraries performing the same job.

Do not remove a required dependency solely because it increases APK size.

### 7. Release Optimization Gate

Before requesting approval for a new EAS build:

- All critical business workflows must pass tests.
- TypeScript, ESLint, and Expo Doctor must pass.
- Check for unused dependencies and accidentally bundled development assets.
- Confirm appropriate release minification and resource shrinking.
- Confirm security and database functionality remain intact.
- Summarize any known performance concerns.

After an approved EAS build, report:

- Actual APK file size
- Build profile and Git commit
- APK architecture(s), if verified
- Startup performance, if measured
- Relevant performance regressions
- Prioritized optimization opportunities

Never invent benchmark results.

### 8. Optimization Priorities

Priority order:

1. Financial correctness and data integrity
2. Security
3. Functional offline workflows
4. UI responsiveness and Android stability
5. Battery and memory efficiency
6. APK size reduction
7. Cosmetic performance improvements

Do not perform broad architectural rewrites merely to reduce bundle size.

### 9. Automation

Apply these rules to every future CredKeep task automatically.

Use profiling and measurement before making invasive optimizations.

Perform safe, low-risk optimization during development.

Reserve intensive APK analysis and performance tuning for the final V1 stabilization phase.

## Multi-Agent Workflow Integration
This project uses a split-agent model:
- **Codex:** Planner, Tester, Reviewer. Operates independently.
- **Gemini / Antigravity:** Implementer and Orchestrator.

**Coordination Rules:**
1. All agents MUST read `.workflow/STATE.md` before taking action.
2. Agents MUST read `AGENTS.md` before every action.
3. Hand-offs occur by updating `.workflow/HANDOFF.md` and invoking the next agent.
4. Codex is the final arbiter of QA and tests.
5. All completed work MUST have local Git commits with conventional commit messages.

## graphify

This project has a knowledge graph at graphify-out/ with god nodes, community structure, and cross-file relationships.

When the user types `/graphify`, use the installed graphify skill or instructions before doing anything else.

Rules:
- For codebase questions, first run `graphify query "<question>"` when graphify-out/graph.json exists. Use `graphify path "<A>" "<B>"` for relationships and `graphify explain "<concept>"` for focused concepts. These return a scoped subgraph, usually much smaller than GRAPH_REPORT.md or raw grep output.
- Dirty graphify-out/ files are expected after hooks or incremental updates; dirty graph files are not a reason to skip graphify. Only skip graphify if the task is about stale or incorrect graph output, or the user explicitly says not to use it.
- If graphify-out/wiki/index.md exists, use it for broad navigation instead of raw source browsing.
- Read graphify-out/GRAPH_REPORT.md only for broad architecture review or when query/path/explain do not surface enough context.
- After modifying code, run `graphify update .` to keep the graph current (AST-only, no API cost).

name: diagnosing-bugs
description: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says "diagnose"/"debug this", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.
---

# Diagnosing Bugs

A discipline for hard bugs. Skip phases only when explicitly justified.

When exploring the codebase, read `GLOSSARY.md` (if it exists) to get a clear mental model of the relevant modules, and check ADRs in the area you're touching.

## Redact

This skill has you show commands, outputs and captured artifacts. **Redact every secret first**: write `<REDACTED>` in its place. Build loops against env vars, so the credential stays in the environment rather than in what you show. Captured artifacts carry auth headers: quote only the lines that carry the signal.

If the redacted output is not enough to diagnose the bug, say so and ask the user.

## Phase 1: Build a feedback loop

**This is the skill.** Everything else is mechanical. If you have a **tight** pass/fail signal for the bug (one that goes red on _this_ bug), you will find the cause; bisection, hypothesis-testing, and instrumentation all just consume it. If you don't have one, no amount of staring at code will save you.

Spend disproportionate effort here. **Be aggressive. Be creative. Refuse to give up.**

### Ways to construct one, in roughly this order

1. **Failing test** at whatever seam reaches the bug: unit, integration, e2e.
2. **Curl / HTTP script** against a running dev server.
3. **CLI invocation** with a fixture input, diffing stdout against a known-good snapshot.
4. **Headless browser script** (Playwright / Puppeteer) that drives the UI and asserts on DOM/console/network.
5. **Replay a captured trace.** Save a real network request / payload / event log to disk; replay it through the code path in isolation.
6. **Throwaway harness.** Spin up a minimal subset of the system (one service, mocked deps) that exercises the bug code path with a single function call.
7. **Property / fuzz loop.** If the bug is "sometimes wrong output", run 1000 random inputs and look for the failure mode.
8. **Bisection harness.** If the bug appeared between two known states (commit, dataset, version), automate "boot at state X, check, repeat" so you can `git bisect run` it.
9. **Differential loop.** Run the same input through old-version vs new-version (or two configs) and diff outputs.
10. **HITL bash script.** Last resort. If a human must click, drive _them_ with `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh` so the loop is still structured. Captured output feeds back to you.

Build the right feedback loop, and the bug is 90% fixed.

### Tighten the loop

Treat the loop as a product. Once you have _a_ loop, **tighten** it:

- Can I make it faster? (Cache setup, skip unrelated init, narrow the test scope.)
- Can I make the signal sharper? (Assert on the specific symptom, not "didn't crash".)
- Can I make it more deterministic? (Pin time, seed RNG, isolate filesystem, freeze network.)

A 30-second flaky loop is barely better than no loop; a 2-second deterministic one is tight, a debugging superpower.

### Non-deterministic bugs

The goal is not a clean repro but a **higher reproduction rate**. Loop the trigger 100Ã—, parallelise, add stress, narrow timing windows, inject sleeps. A 50%-flake bug is debuggable; 1% is not, so keep raising the rate until it's debuggable.

### When you genuinely cannot build a loop

Stop and say so explicitly. List what you tried. Ask the user for: (a) access to whatever environment reproduces it, (b) a redacted captured artifact (HAR file, log dump, core dump, screen recording with timestamps), or (c) permission to add temporary production instrumentation. Do **not** proceed to hypothesise without a loop.

### Completion criterion: a tight loop that goes red

Phase 1 is done when the loop is **tight** and **red-capable**: you can name **one command** (a script path, a test invocation, a curl) that you have **already run at least once** (show the invocation and its output, redacted), and that is:

- [ ] **Red-capable**: it drives the actual bug code path and asserts the **user's exact symptom**, so it can go red on this bug and green once fixed. Not "runs without erroring"; it must be able to _catch this specific bug_.
- [ ] **Deterministic**: same verdict every run (flaky bugs: a pinned, high reproduction rate, per above).
- [ ] **Fast**: seconds, not minutes.
- [ ] **Agent-runnable**: you can run it unattended; a human in the loop only via `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh`.

If you catch yourself reading code to build a theory before this command exists, **stop: jumping straight to a hypothesis is the exact failure this skill prevents.** No red-capable command, no Phase 2.

## Phase 2: Reproduce + minimise

Run the loop. Watch it go red as the bug appears.

Confirm:

- [ ] The loop produces the failure mode the **user** described, not a different failure that happens to be nearby. Wrong bug = wrong fix.
- [ ] The failure is reproducible across multiple runs (or, for non-deterministic bugs, reproducible at a high enough rate to debug against).
- [ ] You have captured the exact symptom (error message, wrong output, slow timing) so later phases can verify the fix actually addresses it.

### Minimise

Once it's red, shrink the repro to the **smallest scenario that still goes red**. Cut inputs, callers, config, data, and steps **one at a time**, re-running the loop after each cut, and keep only what's load-bearing for the failure.

Why bother: a minimal repro shrinks the hypothesis space in Phase 3 (fewer moving parts left to suspect) and becomes the clean regression test in Phase 5.

Done when **every remaining element is load-bearing**: removing any one of them makes the loop go green.

Do not proceed until you have reproduced **and** minimised.

## Phase 3: Hypothesise

Generate **3â€“5 ranked hypotheses** before testing any of them. Single-hypothesis generation anchors on the first plausible idea.

Each hypothesis must be **falsifiable**: state the prediction it makes.

> Format: "If <X> is the cause, then <changing Y> will make the bug disappear / <changing Z> will make it worse."

If you cannot state the prediction, the hypothesis is a vibe: discard or sharpen it.

**Show the ranked list to the user before testing.** They often have domain knowledge that re-ranks instantly ("we just deployed a change to #3"), or know hypotheses they've already ruled out. Cheap checkpoint, big time saver. Don't block on it; proceed with your ranking if the user is AFK.

## Phase 4: Instrument

Each probe must map to a specific prediction from Phase 3. **Change one variable at a time.**

Tool preference:

1. **Debugger / REPL inspection** if the env supports it. One breakpoint beats ten logs.
2. **Targeted logs** at the boundaries that distinguish hypotheses.
3. Never "log everything and grep".

**Tag every debug log** with a unique prefix, e.g. `[DEBUG-a4f2]`. Cleanup at the end becomes a single grep. Untagged logs survive; tagged logs die.

**Perf branch.** For performance regressions, logs are usually wrong. Instead: establish a baseline measurement (timing harness, `performance.now()`, profiler, query plan), then bisect. Measure first, fix second.

## Phase 5: Fix + regression test

Write the regression test **before the fix**, but only if there is a **correct seam** for it.

A correct seam is one where the test exercises the **real bug pattern** as it occurs at the call site. If the only available seam is too shallow (single-caller test when the bug needs multiple callers, unit test that can't replicate the chain that triggered the bug), a regression test there gives false confidence.

**If no correct seam exists, that itself is the finding.** Note it. The codebase architecture is preventing the bug from being locked down. Flag this for the next phase.

If a correct seam exists:

1. Turn the minimised repro into a failing test at that seam.
2. Watch it fail.
3. Apply the fix.
4. Watch it pass.
5. Re-run the Phase 1 feedback loop against the original (un-minimised) scenario.

## Phase 6: Cleanup

Required before declaring done:

- [ ] Original repro no longer reproduces (re-run the Phase 1 loop)
- [ ] Regression test passes (or absence of seam is documented)
- [ ] All `[DEBUG-...]` instrumentation removed (`grep` the prefix)
- [ ] Throwaway prototypes deleted (or moved to a clearly-marked debug location)
- [ ] The hypothesis that turned out correct is stated in the commit / PR message, so the next debugger learns

GLOSSARY.md:40:A financing model where the Borrower pays a fixed monthly service charge/markup periodically, and repays the entire principal in a single bullet payment at maturity.
GLOSSARY.md:45:_Avoid_: EMI, installment, payment slice
GLOSSARY.md:63:### Repayments, Penalties & Overdue Collections
GLOSSARY.md:65:**Collection Receipt**:
GLOSSARY.md:67:_Avoid_: Payment slip, voucher, credit slip
GLOSSARY.md:70:The deterministic sequence used to apply incoming collection funds across accrued Late Penalties, due Markup, and Principal.
GLOSSARY.md:71:_Avoid_: Payment splitting, balance deduction
GLOSSARY.md:73:**Partial Payment**:
GLOSSARY.md:74:A collection where the amount received is less than the currently due Kist plus accrued penalties.
GLOSSARY.md:75:_Avoid_: Half kist, split payment
GLOSSARY.md:78:The immediate automatic allocation of excess collection cash toward the earliest unpaid future scheduled Kists without creating a separate advance wallet.
GLOSSARY.md:79:_Avoid_: Advance payment, wallet credit, overpayment buffer
GLOSSARY.md:81:**Unallocated Surplus**:
GLOSSARY.md:90:A flat fee of ₹100 per calendar day assessed once per overdue Loan Account starting the day after the earliest unpaid due date. Cleared upon payment and never carried into future months.
GLOSSARY.md:94:An explicit, recorded noncash action by the financier to forgive an accrued Late Penalty without modifying principal, markup, or cash collections.

docs\FINANCIAL_RULES.md:138:1. **On-Time Payment**: Pay ₹2,600 on due date &rarr; Kist marked `PAID`, ₹0 penalty, next month starts clean at ₹2,600.
docs\FINANCIAL_RULES.md:140:3. **Independent Consecutive Late Payments**: Month 1 paid 5 days late (₹3,100 collected); Month 2 paid 5 days late &rarr; independently payable as ₹3,100 (₹500 penalty), no penalty carried over from Month 1.

docs\FINANCIAL_RULES.md:145:8. **Partial Payment**: ₹1,000 paid against ₹2,600 kist &rarr; Kist 1 `PARTIALLY_PAID`, ₹1,600 remaining due.
docs\FINANCIAL_RULES.md:151:14. **Payment Reversal (Latest Only)**: Reverse payment that paid K1 and ₹100 of K2 &rarr; K1 and K2 restore exact prior unpaid balances; DPD recalculates cleanly.

docs\FINANCIAL_RULES.md:169:     1. Apply incoming payments toward the oldest unpaid scheduled instalment first.

.scratch/v1-financial-engine\issues\06-sqlite-schema-and-idempotency.md:9:- **Defect in ADR-0003 `transactions` table DDL**: The schema specification mistakenly declares two primary keys: `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL` and `sequence_number INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT`. SQLite rejects multiple primary key declarations with `table "transactions" has more than one primary key`, and restricts `AUTOINCREMENT` strictly to `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`.
.scratch/v1-financial-engine\issues\06-sqlite-schema-and-idempotency.md:16:- Creates normalized schema: `borrowers`, `loans`, `repayment_schedules`, `transactions`, `loan_overdue_periods`, `schedule_modifications`.
.scratch/v1-financial-engine\issues\06-sqlite-schema-and-idempotency.md:17:- Triggers prevent runtime `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `transactions`.
.scratch/v1-financial-engine\issues\06-sqlite-schema-and-idempotency.md:19:- `UNIQUE INDEX idx_transactions_operation_id` prevents duplicate mutations across app crashes and retries.
docs\adr\0002-append-only-double-entry-ledger.md:6:Earlier planning proposed a full event-sourced double-entry accounting engine with SHA-256 cryptographic hash chaining for each collection receipt, emulating distributed ledgers.
docs\adr\0002-append-only-double-entry-ledger.md:9:1. **Unnecessary Complexity**: For a single-financier offline ledger, full event-sourcing with event reducers adds substantial query latency, complex snapshotting, and difficult schema migrations without providing business value over relational tables with immutable transaction journals.
docs\adr\0002-append-only-double-entry-ledger.md:11:3. **Auditability Without Overkill**: Single-financier compliance requires immutable transaction logs, explicit correction records with timestamps, and foreign-key integrity, which relational tables provide naturally.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:7:1. Fast indexed dashboard and borrower queries without computing entire account histories from raw transaction logs on every render.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:8:2. An auditable, append-only financial ledger where no historic transaction can be deleted or silently modified at runtime.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:9:3. Persistent protection against duplicate payment entries across application crashes, background process kills, or rapid user retries.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:11:5. Direct sequential allocation of extra payments to future scheduled kists without exposing a complex advance-wallet interface.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:13:7. Crash resilience ensuring an unexpected device shutdown or power loss mid-operation leaves zero inconsistent states between materialized balances and transaction logs.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:19:We implement a normalized relational financial schema using SQLite. The design combines **immutable transaction records** with **materialized balance columns**, governed by database-level constraints, atomic transaction blocks, and deterministic in-memory financial domain logic.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:41:  Never execute manual SQL strings such as `BEGIN TRANSACTION`, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, or `COMMIT` directly inside `withExclusiveTransactionAsync`. In `expo-sqlite`, transaction lifecycle and locking are managed by the native bridge wrapper; manual transaction SQL causes internal state collisions and crashes.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:49:The `transactions` table represents the canonical journal of financial events (Disbursements, Collections, Penalties, Waivers, Adjustments, Reversals, Write-Offs):
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:53:  CREATE TRIGGER prevent_transactions_update
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:54:  BEFORE UPDATE ON transactions
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:59:  CREATE TRIGGER prevent_transactions_delete
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:60:  BEFORE DELETE ON transactions
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:70:### 3. Persistent Database-Enforced Payment Idempotency
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:74:- The `transactions` table enforces a persistent unique index:
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:76:  CREATE TABLE transactions (
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:81:    transaction_type TEXT NOT NULL, -- 'DISBURSEMENT', 'COLLECTION', 'PENALTY_ASSESSMENT', 'PENALTY_WAIVER', 'WRITE_OFF', 'REFUND', 'COLLECTION_REVERSAL'
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:83:    payment_method TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CASH', -- 'CASH', 'UPI', 'BANK_TRANSFER'
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:85:    allocated_principal_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:86:    allocated_markup_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:87:    allocated_penalty_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:88:    unallocated_surplus_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:97:  CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_transactions_operation_id ON transactions(operation_id);
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:100:  If a duplicate submission occurs (e.g., following an app restart after an interrupted response or double-tap), SQLite raises a `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE` violation. The repository intercepts this error, queries the existing transaction by `operation_id`, and safely returns the existing record without mutating balances.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:120:### 5. Extra Payments & Sequential Future Kist Reduction
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:124:- In `repayment_schedules`:
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:126:  CREATE TABLE repayment_schedules (
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:141:- Extra payments directly increase `principal_paid_paise` and `markup_paid_paise` on future kists. The future kist displays its remaining net payable balance without changing its scheduled due date.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:142:- Any payment exceeding the total face value of the entire loan is routed to `unallocated_surplus_paise` for refunding.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:163:    FOREIGN KEY (kist_id) REFERENCES repayment_schedules(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:166:- Only the target kist's `effective_due_date` updates in `repayment_schedules`. Subsequent kist due dates remain intact.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:167:- If `accrued_penalty_action = 'WAIVE'`, a noncash `PENALTY_WAIVER` transaction row is inserted in the same atomic transaction, reducing `outstanding_penalty_paise`.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:176:   $$\text{TotalCashCollected} = \sum_{\substack{T \in \text{transactions} \\ T.\text{transaction\_type} = \text{'COLLECTION'}}} T.\text{amount\_paise} - \sum_{\substack{R \in \text{transactions} \\ R.\text{transaction\_type} = \text{'REFUND'}}} R.\text{amount\_paise}$$
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:197:### 8. Historical Payment Reversal Policy (LIFO)
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:199:1. **Latest-Payment Reversal Only (LIFO)**:
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:200:   In V1, **only the single latest active payment on a specific loan account may be reversed**.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:202:   If a financier attempts to reverse an older payment when subsequent payments exist on that loan account, the engine **strictly rejects the request** with error code `CANNOT_REVERSE_HISTORICAL_PAYMENT`. The application will not perform silent cascading reallocations or multi-transaction rewrites.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:204:   The reversal inserts a compensating transaction of type `COLLECTION_REVERSAL`:
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:215:1. **On-Time Payment**: Full payment of ₹2,600 on due date &rarr; status `PAID`, ₹0 penalty, next month starts clean at ₹2,600.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:217:3. **Independent Consecutive Late Payments**: Month 1 paid 5 days late (₹3,100 collected); Month 2 paid 5 days late &rarr; independently payable as ₹3,100; zero penalty carried from Month 1.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:220:6. **Surplus Payment to Next Kist**: ₹2,700 paid on ₹2,600 kist &rarr; Kist 1 `PAID` (₹2,600), Kist 2 remaining payable is ₹2,500 due on its original scheduled date.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:222:8. **Partial Payment**: ₹1,000 paid against ₹2,600 kist &rarr; Kist 1 `PARTIALLY_PAID`, ₹1,600 remaining due.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:228:14. **Payment Reversal (Latest Only)**: Reverse payment that paid K1 and ₹100 of K2 &rarr; both kists and DPD restore to exact prior states.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:231:    - *Exception Rollback*: Verify transaction rollback on runtime exception.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:238:  - Direct mental model aligns with real-world field collections.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:241:  - Zero duplicate penalties or double-collections via database-enforced idempotency keys.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:245:  - Reversals in V1 are constrained to the latest payment (LIFO), preventing arbitrary out-of-order historical edits.
docs\adr\0003-relational-ledger-with-audit-history.md:246:  - Materialized balances require strict discipline to update only within exclusive transactions alongside journal rows.

import React, { useState, useEffect, useCallback, useMemo } from 'react';
import { View, StyleSheet, Alert, ScrollView, FlatList, TouchableOpacity } from 'react-native';
import { SafeAreaView } from 'react-native-safe-area-context';
import * as crypto from 'expo-crypto';
import { Typography } from '../../ui/Typography';
import { Button } from '../../ui/Button';
import { Card } from '../../ui/Card';
import { Input } from '../../ui/Input';
import { theme } from '../../ui/theme';
import { useDatabase } from '../../core/database/DatabaseProvider';
import { Loan } from '../../core/database/loanRepository';
import { Borrower } from '../../core/database/borrowerRepository';
import { Paise, formatINR } from '../../core/domain/money';

export default function CollectionsScreen() {
  const { loans, transactions, borrowers, refreshVersion, triggerRefresh } = useDatabase();
  const [loansList, setLoansList] = useState<Loan[]>([]);
  const [borrowersList, setBorrowersList] = useState<Borrower[]>([]);
  
  const [selectedLoanId, setSelectedLoanId] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [amountInr, setAmountInr] = useState('');
  const [result, setResult] = useState<any>(null);

  const [previewPenalty, setPreviewPenalty] = useState<number>(0);
  const [outstandingPenalty, setOutstandingPenalty] = useState<number>(0);

  const loadData = useCallback(async () => {
    if (loans && borrowers) {
      setLoansList(await loans.getAll());
      setBorrowersList(await borrowers.getAll());
    }
  }, [loans, borrowers]);

  useEffect(() => {
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/set-state-in-effect
    loadData();
  }, [loadData, refreshVersion]);

  useEffect(() => {
    async function fetchPenalty() {
      if (selectedLoanId && transactions) {
        const currentDate = new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0];
        const newPen = await transactions.previewPenalties(selectedLoanId, currentDate);
        setPreviewPenalty(newPen);
        
        const summary = await transactions.getEarningsSummary({ loanId: selectedLoanId });
        setOutstandingPenalty(summary.outstandingPenalty);
      }
    }
    fetchPenalty();
  }, [selectedLoanId, transactions, refreshVersion]);

  const activeLoans = useMemo(() => loansList.filter(l => l.status === 'ACTIVE'), [loansList]);

  const getBorrowerName = (id: string) => {
    return borrowersList.find(b => b.id === id)?.name || 'Unknown Borrower';
  };

  const handlePayment = async () => {
    if (!selectedLoanId || !amountInr) {
      Alert.alert('Error', 'Please enter an amount');
      return;
    }
    
    try {
      const amountPaise = (parseInt(amountInr) * 100) as Paise;
      const effectiveDate = new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0];
      const operationId = crypto.randomUUID();
      
      const res = await transactions!.recordCashPayment(
        selectedLoanId,
        amountPaise,
        operationId,
        effectiveDate
      );
      
      setResult(res);
      setAmountInr('');
      Alert.alert('Success', 'Payment recorded successfully!');
      triggerRefresh();
    } catch (e: any) {
      Alert.alert('Error', e.message);
    }
  };

  if (!selectedLoanId) {
    return (
      <SafeAreaView style={styles.container} edges={['top']}>
        <View style={styles.header}>
          <Typography variant="headlineMd" color={theme.colors.onPrimary}>Select Loan for Collection</Typography>
        </View>
        <FlatList
          data={activeLoans}
          keyExtractor={item => item.id}
          contentContainerStyle={styles.listContent}
          renderItem={({ item }) => (
            <TouchableOpacity onPress={() => setSelectedLoanId(item.id)}>
              <Card elevated style={styles.loanCard}>
                <Typography variant="headlineSm">{getBorrowerName(item.borrower_id)}</Typography>
                <Typography variant="bodyMd">Principal: {formatINR(item.principal_paise as Paise)}</Typography>
                <Typography variant="bodySm" color={theme.colors.textSecondary}>Loan ID: {item.id.substring(0, 8)}</Typography>
              </Card>
            </TouchableOpacity>
          )}
          ListEmptyComponent={
            <View style={styles.empty}>
              <Typography variant="bodyLg" color={theme.colors.textSecondary}>No active loans found</Typography>
            </View>
          }
        />
      </SafeAreaView>
    );
  }

  const selectedLoan = loansList.find(l => l.id === selectedLoanId);
  const totalPenalty = previewPenalty + outstandingPenalty;

  return (
    <SafeAreaView style={styles.container} edges={['top']}>
    <ScrollView contentContainerStyle={styles.content}>
      <View style={styles.row}>
        <TouchableOpacity onPress={() => { setSelectedLoanId(null); setResult(null); }}>
          <Typography color={theme.colors.primary}>â† Back</Typography>
        </TouchableOpacity>
      </View>
      
      <Typography variant="headlineMd" style={styles.title}>Record Collection</Typography>
      
      {selectedLoan && (
        <Card style={styles.selectedLoanCard}>
          <Typography variant="labelLg">Borrower</Typography>
          <Typography variant="headlineSm">{getBorrowerName(selectedLoan.borrower_id)}</Typography>
          <Typography variant="bodyMd" style={{marginTop: 8}}>Principal: {formatINR(selectedLoan.principal_paise as Paise)}</Typography>
          
          <View style={{marginTop: 12, padding: 8, backgroundColor: theme.colors.errorContainer, borderRadius: 8}}>
            <Typography variant="labelMd" color={theme.colors.error}>Penalty Preview</Typography>
            <Typography variant="bodySm" color={theme.colors.error}>
              Outstanding: {formatINR(outstandingPenalty as Paise)}
            </Typography>
            <Typography variant="bodySm" color={theme.colors.error}>
              New (Unassessed): {formatINR(previewPenalty as Paise)}
            </Typography>
            <Typography variant="labelLg" color={theme.colors.error} style={{marginTop: 4}}>
              Total Penalty Due: {formatINR(totalPenalty as Paise)}
            </Typography>
          </View>
        </Card>
      )}

      <Card elevated style={styles.card}>
        <Input
          label="Amount Received (INR)"
          keyboardType="numeric"
          placeholder="e.g. 2600"
          value={amountInr}
          onChangeText={setAmountInr}
        />

        <Button title="Record Payment" onPress={handlePayment} style={styles.button} />
      </Card>

      {result && (
        <Card style={styles.resultCard}>
          <Typography variant="headlineSm">Payment Allocated</Typography>
          <Typography variant="bodySm">Transaction ID: {result.transactionId.substring(0,8)}</Typography>
          <Typography variant="bodySm">Unallocated: {formatINR((result.result?.unallocatedFunds || 0) as Paise)}</Typography>
          <Typography variant="bodySm" color={theme.colors.error}>
             Remaining Penalty: {formatINR((result.result?.remainingPenalty || 0) as Paise)}
          </Typography>
          {result.result?.allocations?.map((a: any, i: number) => (
            <Typography key={i} variant="bodySm" color={theme.colors.success}>
              {a.allocatedToKist > 0 
                ? `Kist #${a.allocatedToKist}: ${formatINR((a.allocatedToPrincipal + a.allocatedToMarkup) as Paise)}`
                : `Penalty: ${formatINR(a.allocatedToPenalty as Paise)}`
              }
            </Typography>
          ))}
        </Card>
      )}
    </ScrollView>
    </SafeAreaView>
  );
}

const styles = StyleSheet.create({
  container: { flex: 1, backgroundColor: theme.colors.background },
  header: { padding: theme.spacing.lg, backgroundColor: theme.colors.primary, paddingTop: 16 },
  listContent: { padding: theme.spacing.md, gap: theme.spacing.md },
  content: { padding: theme.spacing.lg },
  row: { flexDirection: 'row', marginBottom: theme.spacing.md },
  title: { marginBottom: theme.spacing.lg },
  card: { padding: theme.spacing.lg, marginBottom: theme.spacing.lg },
  loanCard: { marginBottom: theme.spacing.sm },
  selectedLoanCard: { backgroundColor: theme.colors.surfaceTint, padding: theme.spacing.md, marginBottom: theme.spacing.lg },
  empty: { padding: theme.spacing.xl, alignItems: 'center' },
  button: { marginTop: theme.spacing.md },
  resultCard: { backgroundColor: theme.colors.successContainer, borderColor: theme.colors.success, marginTop: theme.spacing.md }
});

import { SQLiteDatabase } from 'expo-sqlite';
import * as crypto from 'expo-crypto';
import { allocatePayment, KistAllocationState } from '../domain/allocation';
import { Paise } from '../domain/money';

export class TransactionRepository {
  constructor(private db: SQLiteDatabase) {}

  async recordCashPayment(
    loanId: string, 
    paymentAmount: Paise, 
    operationId: string,
    effectiveDate: string
  ) {
      let returnResult: any = null;

      await this.db.withExclusiveTransactionAsync(async (tx) => {
        // Idempotency check
        const existing = await this.db.getFirstAsync(`SELECT * FROM transactions WHERE operation_id = ?`, [operationId]);
        if (existing) {
          returnResult = existing;
          return;
        }
        
        // Ensure schedule is up to date (for bullet loans)
        const { LoanRepository } = require('./loanRepository');
        const loanRepo = new LoanRepository(this.db);
        await loanRepo.extendScheduleIfNeeded(loanId);

        // Load current kists
        const dbKists = await this.db.getAllAsync<any>(
          `SELECT * FROM repayment_schedules WHERE loan_id = ? ORDER BY installment_number ASC`, 
          [loanId]
        );
        
        const kists: KistAllocationState[] = dbKists.map(k => ({
          kistNumber: k.installment_number,
          amountDue: k.total_due_paise as Paise,
          principalDue: k.principal_component_paise as Paise,
          markupDue: k.markup_component_paise as Paise,
          amountPaid: (k.principal_paid_paise + k.markup_paid_paise) as Paise,
          principalPaid: k.principal_paid_paise as Paise,
          markupPaid: k.markup_paid_paise as Paise,
          status: k.status,
          isDue: k.effective_due_date <= effectiveDate
        }));
        
        // Assess penalties up to effectiveDate
        await this.assessPenalties(loanId, effectiveDate);

        // Fetch outstanding penalty
        // Outstanding penalty = (Total PENALTY_ASSESSMENT amounts) - (Total allocated_penalty_paise from all collections)
        const penaltyAssessments = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(
          `SELECT SUM(amount_paise) as total FROM transactions WHERE loan_id = ? AND transaction_type = 'PENALTY_ASSESSMENT'`,
          [loanId]
        );
        const penaltyPaid = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(
          `SELECT SUM(allocated_penalty_paise) as total FROM transactions WHERE loan_id = ? AND transaction_type = 'COLLECTION'`,
          [loanId]
        );
        
        const totalPenaltyAssessed = (penaltyAssessments?.total || 0) as number;
        const totalPenaltyPaid = (penaltyPaid?.total || 0) as number;
        const outstandingPenalty = Math.max(0, totalPenaltyAssessed - totalPenaltyPaid) as Paise;
  
        const result = allocatePayment({ paymentAmount, kists, outstandingPenalty });
        
        const transactionId = crypto.randomUUID();
        const createdAt = new Date().toISOString();
        
        let allocatedPrincipal = 0;
        let allocatedMarkup = 0;
        let allocatedPenalty = 0;
        
        for (const a of result.allocations) {
          allocatedPrincipal += a.allocatedToPrincipal;
          allocatedMarkup += a.allocatedToMarkup;
          allocatedPenalty += a.allocatedToPenalty;
        }
        
        await this.db.runAsync(`
          INSERT INTO transactions (
            id, operation_id, loan_id, transaction_type, is_cash, payment_method, 
            amount_paise, allocated_principal_paise, allocated_markup_paise, 
            allocated_penalty_paise, unallocated_surplus_paise, effective_date, created_at
          ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
        `, [
          transactionId, operationId, loanId, 'COLLECTION', 1, 'CASH',
          paymentAmount, allocatedPrincipal, allocatedMarkup, allocatedPenalty,
          result.unallocatedFunds, effectiveDate, createdAt
        ]);
        
        // Update repayment schedules
        const updateStmt = await this.db.prepareAsync(`
          UPDATE repayment_schedules
          SET principal_paid_paise = ?, markup_paid_paise = ?, status = ?
          WHERE loan_id = ? AND installment_number = ?
        `);
        
        try {
          for (const k of result.updatedKists) {
            // Only update if changed
            const orig = kists.find(o => o.kistNumber === k.kistNumber)!;
            if (k.amountPaid !== orig.amountPaid || k.status !== orig.status) {
               await updateStmt.executeAsync([
                 k.principalPaid, k.markupPaid, k.status || 'PENDING', loanId, k.kistNumber
               ]);
            }
          }
        } finally {
          await updateStmt.finalizeAsync();
        }
        // Check if loan should be closed
        const loan = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(`SELECT product_type FROM loans WHERE id = ?`, [loanId]);
        
        let allKistsPaid = true;
        for (const k of result.updatedKists) {
          if (k.status !== 'PAID') {
            allKistsPaid = false;
            break;
          }
        }
        
        // For instalment loans, if all kists are paid and no outstanding penalty remains, settle the loan
        // For bullet loans, they require an explicit principal repayment transaction to settle (not yet implemented in this method)
        if (loan && loan.product_type === 'instalment' && allKistsPaid && result.remainingPenalty <= 0) {
           await this.db.runAsync(`UPDATE loans SET status = 'SETTLED' WHERE id = ?`, [loanId]);
        }
        
        returnResult = { success: true, transactionId, result };
      });
      
      return returnResult;
  }

  async previewPenalties(loanId: string, currentDate: string): Promise<number> {
    const loan = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(`SELECT * FROM loans WHERE id = ?`, [loanId]);
    if (!loan || loan.status !== 'ACTIVE') return 0;

    const { LoanRepository } = require('./loanRepository');
    const loanRepo = new LoanRepository(this.db);
    await loanRepo.extendScheduleIfNeeded(loanId);

    const dbKists = await this.db.getAllAsync<any>(
      `SELECT * FROM repayment_schedules WHERE loan_id = ? ORDER BY installment_number ASC`,
      [loanId]
    );

    const assessments = await this.db.getAllAsync<any>(
      `SELECT effective_date FROM transactions WHERE loan_id = ? AND transaction_type = 'PENALTY_ASSESSMENT'`,
      [loanId]
    );

    const assessedDates = new Set<string>(assessments.map(a => a.effective_date));

    // Dynamic import to avoid circular dep
    const { calculateNewPenalties } = require('../domain/penalty');
    const { toPaise } = require('../domain/money');

    const state = {
      loanId,
      rule: { dailyRatePaise: toPaise(100) },
      kists: dbKists.map(k => ({
        kistNumber: k.installment_number,
        dueDate: k.effective_due_date,
        amountDue: k.total_due_paise,
        amountPaid: k.principal_paid_paise + k.markup_paid_paise,
        isFullyPaid: k.status === 'PAID'
      })),
      assessedDates
    };

    const { newPenaltyAmount } = calculateNewPenalties(state, currentDate);
    return newPenaltyAmount;
  }

  async assessPenalties(loanId: string, currentDate: string): Promise<number> {
    const loan = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(`SELECT * FROM loans WHERE id = ?`, [loanId]);
    if (!loan || loan.status !== 'ACTIVE') return 0;

    const { LoanRepository } = require('./loanRepository');
    const loanRepo = new LoanRepository(this.db);
    await loanRepo.extendScheduleIfNeeded(loanId);

    const dbKists = await this.db.getAllAsync<any>(
      `SELECT * FROM repayment_schedules WHERE loan_id = ? ORDER BY installment_number ASC`,
      [loanId]
    );

    const assessments = await this.db.getAllAsync<any>(
      `SELECT effective_date FROM transactions WHERE loan_id = ? AND transaction_type = 'PENALTY_ASSESSMENT'`,
      [loanId]
    );

    const assessedDates = new Set<string>(assessments.map(a => a.effective_date));

    // Dynamic import to avoid circular dep if any, or just require
    const { calculateNewPenalties, LoanPenaltyState } = require('../domain/penalty');
    const { toPaise } = require('../domain/money');

    const state = {
      loanId,
      rule: { dailyRatePaise: toPaise(100) },
      kists: dbKists.map(k => ({
        kistNumber: k.installment_number,
        dueDate: k.effective_due_date,
        amountDue: k.total_due_paise,
        amountPaid: k.principal_paid_paise + k.markup_paid_paise,
        isFullyPaid: k.status === 'PAID'
      })),
      assessedDates
    };

    const { newPenaltyAmount, newAssessedDates } = calculateNewPenalties(state, currentDate);

    if (newPenaltyAmount > 0) {
      for (const date of newAssessedDates) {
        const transactionId = crypto.randomUUID();
        const operationId = `penalty-${loanId}-${date}`;
        
        // Check if it already exists to be safe
        const existing = await this.db.getFirstAsync(`SELECT id FROM transactions WHERE operation_id = ?`, [operationId]);
        if (!existing) {
          await this.db.runAsync(`
            INSERT INTO transactions (
              id, operation_id, loan_id, transaction_type, is_cash, payment_method, 
              amount_paise, allocated_principal_paise, allocated_markup_paise, 
              allocated_penalty_paise, unallocated_surplus_paise, effective_date, created_at
            ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
          `, [
            transactionId, operationId, loanId, 'PENALTY_ASSESSMENT', 0, 'SYSTEM',
            toPaise(100), 0, 0, 0, 0, date, new Date().toISOString()
          ]);
        }
      }
    }

    return newPenaltyAmount;
  }

  async getEarningsSummary(filter?: { loanId?: string, borrowerId?: string, loanStatus?: string }) {
    let whereClauses: string[] = [];
    let params: any[] = [];

    if (filter?.loanId) {
      whereClauses.push('t.loan_id = ?');
      params.push(filter.loanId);
    }
    if (filter?.borrowerId) {
      whereClauses.push('l.borrower_id = ?');
      params.push(filter.borrowerId);
    }
    if (filter?.loanStatus) {
      whereClauses.push('l.status = ?');
      params.push(filter.loanStatus);
    }

    const whereStr = whereClauses.length > 0 ? `WHERE ${whereClauses.join(' AND ')}` : '';

    // Calculate actual cash received
    const collectedQuery = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(`
      SELECT 
        SUM(t.allocated_principal_paise) as principal_returned,
        SUM(t.allocated_markup_paise) as markup_received,
        SUM(t.allocated_penalty_paise) as penalty_received,
        SUM(t.forgiven_principal_paise) as principal_waived,
        SUM(t.forgiven_markup_paise) as markup_waived,
        SUM(t.forgiven_penalty_paise) as penalty_waived
      FROM transactions t
      JOIN loans l ON t.loan_id = l.id
      ${whereStr} AND t.transaction_type = 'COLLECTION'
    `, params);

    // Calculate outstanding (what is due but not paid)
    // For outstanding penalty, sum PENALTY_ASSESSMENT minus collected penalty
    const penaltyAssessments = await this.db.getFirstAsync<any>(`
      SELECT SUM(t.amount_paise) as total
      FROM transactions t
      JOIN loans l ON t.loan_id = l.id
      ${whereStr} AND t.transaction_type = 'PENALTY_ASSESSMENT'
    `, params);

    const totalPenaltyAssessed = penaltyAssessments?.total || 0;
    const penaltyReceived = collectedQuery?.penalty_received || 0;
    const penaltyWaived = collectedQuery?.penalty_waived || 0;
    const outstandingPenalty = Math.max(0, totalPenaltyAssessed - penaltyReceived - penaltyWaived);

    return {
      principalReturned: collectedQuery?.principal_returned || 0,
      markupReceived: collectedQuery?.markup_received || 0,
      penaltyReceived,
      principalWaived: collectedQuery?.principal_waived || 0,
      markupWaived: collectedQuery?.markup_waived || 0,
      penaltyWaived,
      outstandingPenalty,
      totalEarnings: (collectedQuery?.markup_received || 0) + penaltyReceived
    };
  }
}


codex
The first concrete fault line is now visible: the UI passes one selected `loanId`, but the repository opens an exclusive SQLite transaction and then performs nearly all reads/writes through the parent database handle rather than the transaction-scoped handle. I’m tracing whether that alone explains the reported isolation problem or whether stale UI selection/refresh behavior is an additional cause.

import { Paise, subtractPaise, addPaise } from './money';
import { PaymentAllocation } from './models';

export type KistAllocationState = {
  kistNumber: number;
  amountDue: Paise;
  principalDue: Paise;
  markupDue: Paise;
  amountPaid: Paise;
  principalPaid: Paise;
  markupPaid: Paise;
  isDue?: boolean;
  status?: 'UNPAID' | 'PARTIALLY_PAID' | 'PAID';
};

function allocateWithinKist(
  amount: Paise,
  kist: KistAllocationState
): { p: Paise; m: Paise } {
  const unpaidPrincipal = subtractPaise(kist.principalDue, kist.principalPaid);
  const unpaidMarkup = subtractPaise(kist.markupDue, kist.markupPaid);

  if (amount >= addPaise(unpaidPrincipal, unpaidMarkup)) {
    return { p: unpaidPrincipal, m: unpaidMarkup };
  }

  // Allocate to markup first, then principal
  let m = amount;
  if (m > unpaidMarkup) {
    m = unpaidMarkup;
  }
  
  const p = subtractPaise(amount, m);
  
  return { p, m };
}

export type AllocationInput = {
  paymentAmount: Paise;
  kists: KistAllocationState[];
  outstandingPenalty: Paise;
};

export type AllocationResult = {
  updatedKists: KistAllocationState[];
  allocations: PaymentAllocation[];
  remainingPenalty: Paise;
  unallocatedFunds: Paise;
};

export function allocatePayment(input: AllocationInput): AllocationResult {
  let remainingFunds = input.paymentAmount;
  let remainingPenalty = input.outstandingPenalty;
  
  const updatedKists = input.kists.map(k => ({ 
    ...k, 
    status: k.status || (k.amountPaid >= k.amountDue ? 'PAID' : (k.amountPaid > 0 ? 'PARTIALLY_PAID' : 'UNPAID'))
  }));
  
  const allocations: PaymentAllocation[] = [];
  
  // Phase 1: Due instalments (oldest first)
  for (const kist of updatedKists) {
    if (kist.isDue !== false && kist.status !== 'PAID' && remainingFunds > 0) {
      const remainingDue = subtractPaise(kist.amountDue, kist.amountPaid);
      const allocatedToKist = Math.min(remainingFunds, remainingDue) as Paise;
      
      kist.amountPaid = addPaise(kist.amountPaid, allocatedToKist);
      remainingFunds = subtractPaise(remainingFunds, allocatedToKist);
      
      if (kist.amountPaid >= kist.amountDue) {
        kist.status = 'PAID';
      } else {
        kist.status = 'PARTIALLY_PAID';
      }
      
      if (allocatedToKist > 0) {
        const { p, m } = allocateWithinKist(allocatedToKist, kist);
        kist.principalPaid = addPaise(kist.principalPaid, p);
        kist.markupPaid = addPaise(kist.markupPaid, m);

        allocations.push({
          amount: allocatedToKist,
          allocatedToKist: kist.kistNumber,
          allocatedToPrincipal: p,
          allocatedToMarkup: m,
          allocatedToPenalty: 0 as Paise
        });
      }
    }
  }
  
  // Phase 2: Outstanding penalties
  if (remainingFunds > 0 && remainingPenalty > 0) {
    const allocatedToPenalty = Math.min(remainingFunds, remainingPenalty) as Paise;
    remainingPenalty = subtractPaise(remainingPenalty, allocatedToPenalty);
    remainingFunds = subtractPaise(remainingFunds, allocatedToPenalty);
    
    allocations.push({
      amount: allocatedToPenalty,
      allocatedToKist: 0, // 0 indicates loan-level penalty
      allocatedToPrincipal: 0 as Paise,
      allocatedToMarkup: 0 as Paise,
      allocatedToPenalty: allocatedToPenalty
    });
  }
  
  // Phase 3: Future non-due instalments (pre-payments)
  for (const kist of updatedKists) {
    if (kist.isDue === false && kist.status !== 'PAID' && remainingFunds > 0) {
      const remainingDue = subtractPaise(kist.amountDue, kist.amountPaid);
      const allocatedToKist = Math.min(remainingFunds, remainingDue) as Paise;
      
      kist.amountPaid = addPaise(kist.amountPaid, allocatedToKist);
      remainingFunds = subtractPaise(remainingFunds, allocatedToKist);
      
      if (kist.amountPaid >= kist.amountDue) {
        kist.status = 'PAID';
      } else {
        kist.status = 'PARTIALLY_PAID';
      }
      
      if (allocatedToKist > 0) {
        const { p, m } = allocateWithinKist(allocatedToKist, kist);
        kist.principalPaid = addPaise(kist.principalPaid, p);
        kist.markupPaid = addPaise(kist.markupPaid, m);

        allocations.push({
          amount: allocatedToKist,
          allocatedToKist: kist.kistNumber,
          allocatedToPrincipal: p,
          allocatedToMarkup: m,
          allocatedToPenalty: 0 as Paise
        });
      }
    }
  }

  return {
    updatedKists,
    allocations,
    remainingPenalty,
    unallocatedFunds: remainingFunds
  };
}

import React, { createContext, useContext, useEffect, useState, useCallback } from 'react';
import { SQLiteDatabase } from 'expo-sqlite';
import { initDatabase } from './db';
import { runMigrations } from './schema';
import { BorrowerRepository } from './borrowerRepository';
import { LoanRepository } from './loanRepository';
import { TransactionRepository } from './transactionRepository';

type DatabaseContextType = {
  db: SQLiteDatabase | null;
  borrowers: BorrowerRepository | null;
  loans: LoanRepository | null;
  transactions: TransactionRepository | null;
  error: Error | null;
  refreshVersion: number;
  triggerRefresh: () => void;
};

const DatabaseContext = createContext<DatabaseContextType>({
  db: null,
  borrowers: null,
  loans: null,
  transactions: null,
  error: null,
  refreshVersion: 0,
  triggerRefresh: () => {},
});

export function DatabaseProvider({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  const [refreshVersion, setRefreshVersion] = useState(0);

  const triggerRefresh = useCallback(() => {
    setRefreshVersion(v => v + 1);
  }, []);

  const [state, setState] = useState<Omit<DatabaseContextType, 'refreshVersion' | 'triggerRefresh'>>({
    db: null,
    borrowers: null,
    loans: null,
    transactions: null,
    error: null,
  });

  useEffect(() => {
    async function setup() {
      try {
        const db = await initDatabase();
        await runMigrations(db);
        
        setState({
          db,
          borrowers: new BorrowerRepository(db),
          loans: new LoanRepository(db),
          transactions: new TransactionRepository(db),
          error: null,
        });
      } catch (e: any) {
        setState(prev => ({ ...prev, error: e }));
      }
    }
    setup();
  }, []);

  return (
    <DatabaseContext.Provider value={{ ...state, refreshVersion, triggerRefresh }}>
      {children}
    </DatabaseContext.Provider>
  );
}

export function useDatabase() {
  return useContext(DatabaseContext);
}

import * as SQLite from 'expo-sqlite';
import * as SecureStore from 'expo-secure-store';
import * as Crypto from 'expo-crypto';

const DB_KEY_NAME = 'credkeep_db_master_key';
const DB_NAME = 'credkeep.db';

function bufferToHex(buffer: ArrayBuffer): string {
  return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buffer))
    .map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0'))
    .join('');
}

export async function getDatabaseKey(): Promise<string> {
  let key = await SecureStore.getItemAsync(DB_KEY_NAME);
  
  if (!key) {
    // Generate a 32-byte cryptographically secure random key
    const randomBytes = Crypto.getRandomBytes(32);
    key = bufferToHex(randomBytes.buffer as ArrayBuffer);
    
    await SecureStore.setItemAsync(DB_KEY_NAME, key, {
      requireAuthentication: false,
    });
  }
  
  return key;
}

export async function initDatabase(): Promise<SQLite.SQLiteDatabase> {
  const key = await getDatabaseKey();
  
  // Note: expo-sqlite SDK 50+ natively supports opening databases.
  const db = await SQLite.openDatabaseAsync(DB_NAME);
  
  // PRAGMA configuration
  await db.execAsync(`
    PRAGMA key = "x'${key}'";
    PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
    PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;
    PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
  `);
  
  // Verify encryption by querying something
  await db.getFirstAsync('SELECT 1');
  
  return db;
}

import { SQLiteDatabase } from 'expo-sqlite';

export async function runMigrations(db: SQLiteDatabase) {
  await db.withExclusiveTransactionAsync(async (tx) => {
    // 1. Borrowers table
    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS borrowers (
        id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
        name TEXT NOT NULL,
        phone TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
        created_at TEXT NOT NULL
      );
    `);

    // 2. Loans table
    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS loans (
        id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
        borrower_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        principal_paise INTEGER NOT NULL,
        markup_factor REAL NOT NULL,
        term_months INTEGER NOT NULL,
        start_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        due_day INTEGER NOT NULL,
        product_type TEXT NOT NULL,
        status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ACTIVE',
        created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
        FOREIGN KEY (borrower_id) REFERENCES borrowers(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT
      );
    `);

    // 3. Repayment Schedules
    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS repayment_schedules (
        id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
        loan_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        installment_number INTEGER NOT NULL,
        original_due_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        effective_due_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        principal_component_paise INTEGER NOT NULL,
        markup_component_paise INTEGER NOT NULL,
        total_due_paise INTEGER NOT NULL,
        principal_paid_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        markup_paid_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING',
        FOREIGN KEY (loan_id) REFERENCES loans(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT
      );
    `);

    // 4. Transactions table (Fixing the duplicate PK defect)
    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS transactions (
        id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
        operation_id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
        loan_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        sequence_number INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        transaction_type TEXT NOT NULL,
        is_cash INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
        payment_method TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CASH',
        amount_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        allocated_principal_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        allocated_markup_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        allocated_penalty_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        unallocated_surplus_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        forgiven_principal_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        forgiven_markup_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        forgiven_penalty_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        effective_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
        FOREIGN KEY (loan_id) REFERENCES loans(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT
      );
    `);

    // Prevent transactions update/delete
    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS prevent_transactions_update
      BEFORE UPDATE ON transactions
      BEGIN
        SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'Transactions table is append-only: updates are prohibited');
      END;
    `);

    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS prevent_transactions_delete
      BEFORE DELETE ON transactions
      BEGIN
        SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'Transactions table is append-only: deletions are prohibited');
      END;
    `);

    // 5. Schedule Modifications
    await db.execAsync(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schedule_modifications (
        id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
        loan_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        kist_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        original_due_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        prior_effective_due_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        revised_effective_due_date TEXT NOT NULL,
        changed_at TEXT NOT NULL,
        reason TEXT,
        accrued_penalty_action TEXT NOT NULL,
        waived_penalty_paise INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
        operation_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
        FOREIGN KEY (kist_id) REFERENCES repayment_schedules(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT
      );
    `);
  });
}

import { SQLiteDatabase } from 'expo-sqlite';
import * as crypto from 'expo-crypto';
import { ScheduleOptions, generateSchedule } from '../domain/schedule';

export type Loan = {
  id: string;
  borrower_id: string;
  principal_paise: number;
  markup_factor: number;
  term_months: number;
  start_date: string;
  due_day: number;
  product_type: string;
  status: string;
  created_at: string;
};

export class LoanRepository {
  constructor(private db: SQLiteDatabase) {}

  async createLoanWithSchedule(
    borrowerId: string, 
    options: ScheduleOptions
  ): Promise<Loan> {
    const loanId = crypto.randomUUID();
    const created_at = new Date().toISOString();
    
    const schedule = generateSchedule(options);

    await this.db.withExclusiveTransactionAsync(async (tx) => {
      await this.db.runAsync(
        `INSERT INTO loans (id, borrower_id, principal_paise, markup_factor, term_months, start_date, due_day, product_type, created_at)
         VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`,
        [loanId, borrowerId, options.principal, options.markupFactor, options.termMonths, options.startDate, options.dueDay, options.productType, created_at]
      );
      
      const statement = await this.db.prepareAsync(`
        INSERT INTO repayment_schedules 
        (id, loan_id, installment_number, original_due_date, effective_due_date, principal_component_paise, markup_component_paise, total_due_paise)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
      `);
      
      try {
        for (const kist of schedule) {
          await statement.executeAsync([
            crypto.randomUUID(),
            loanId,
            kist.kistNumber,
            kist.dueDate,
            kist.dueDate,
            kist.principalDue || 0,
            kist.markupDue || 0,
            kist.amountDue
          ]);
        }
      } finally {
        await statement.finalizeAsync();
      }
    });

    return (await this.getById(loanId))!;
  }

  async getAll(): Promise<Loan[]> {
    return await this.db.getAllAsync<Loan>(`SELECT * FROM loans ORDER BY created_at DESC`);
  }
  
  async getByBorrowerId(borrowerId: string): Promise<Loan[]> {
    return await this.db.getAllAsync<Loan>(`SELECT * FROM loans WHERE borrower_id = ? ORDER BY created_at DESC`, [borrowerId]);
  }

  async getById(id: string): Promise<Loan | null> {
    return await this.db.getFirstAsync<Loan>(`SELECT * FROM loans WHERE id = ?`, [id]);
  }
  
  async getSchedule(loanId: string) {
    await this.extendScheduleIfNeeded(loanId);
    return await this.db.getAllAsync(`
      SELECT * FROM repayment_schedules 
      WHERE loan_id = ? 
      ORDER BY installment_number ASC
    `, [loanId]);
  }

  async extendScheduleIfNeeded(loanId: string) {
    const loan = await this.getById(loanId);
    if (!loan || loan.product_type !== 'bullet' || loan.status !== 'ACTIVE') return;

    const kists = await this.db.getAllAsync<any>(`
      SELECT * FROM repayment_schedules 
      WHERE loan_id = ? 
      ORDER BY installment_number ASC
    `, [loanId]);
    
    if (kists.length === 0) return;
    const lastKist = kists[kists.length - 1];
    const currentDate = new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0];
    
    // If the last kist's due date is in the past, generate new ones until we pass currentDate
    if (lastKist.effective_due_date < currentDate) {
      // dynamic import for schedule util if needed, but it's not exported yet? Wait, I exported it.
      const { calculateNextDueDate } = require('../domain/schedule');
      const { toPaise } = require('../domain/money');
      
      const totalMarkup = loan.principal_paise * loan.markup_factor;
      const markupDuePerMonth = Math.floor(totalMarkup / loan.term_months);
      
      let nextInstalmentNumber = lastKist.installment_number + 1;
      let monthsToAddFromStart = nextInstalmentNumber;
      
      let nextDueDate = calculateNextDueDate(loan.start_date, monthsToAddFromStart, loan.due_day);
      
      const statement = await this.db.prepareAsync(`
        INSERT INTO repayment_schedules 
        (id, loan_id, installment_number, original_due_date, effective_due_date, principal_component_paise, markup_component_paise, total_due_paise)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
      `);
      
      try {
        while (nextDueDate <= currentDate || nextInstalmentNumber === lastKist.installment_number + 1 /* always add at least one if we entered */) {
          await statement.executeAsync([
            crypto.randomUUID(),
            loanId,
            nextInstalmentNumber,
            nextDueDate,
            nextDueDate,
            0,
            markupDuePerMonth,
            markupDuePerMonth
          ]);
          
          if (nextDueDate > currentDate) break;
          
          nextInstalmentNumber++;
          monthsToAddFromStart++;
          nextDueDate = calculateNextDueDate(loan.start_date, monthsToAddFromStart, loan.due_day);
        }
      } finally {
        await statement.finalizeAsync();
      }
    }
  }
}

import { SQLiteDatabase } from 'expo-sqlite';
import * as crypto from 'expo-crypto';

export type Borrower = {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  phone: string;
  created_at: string;
};

export class BorrowerRepository {
  constructor(private db: SQLiteDatabase) {}

  async create(name: string, phone: string): Promise<Borrower> {
    const id = crypto.randomUUID();
    const created_at = new Date().toISOString();
    
    await this.db.runAsync(
      `INSERT INTO borrowers (id, name, phone, created_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)`,
      [id, name, phone, created_at]
    );
    
    return { id, name, phone, created_at };
  }

  async getAll(): Promise<Borrower[]> {
    return await this.db.getAllAsync<Borrower>(`SELECT * FROM borrowers ORDER BY name ASC`);
  }

  async getById(id: string): Promise<Borrower | null> {
    return await this.db.getFirstAsync<Borrower>(`SELECT * FROM borrowers WHERE id = ?`, [id]);
  }
}

import { LoanRepository } from './loanRepository';
import { toPaise } from '../domain/money';

describe('LoanRepository Integration', () => {
  let mockDb: any;
  let repo: LoanRepository;
  let queries: any[] = [];
  let statements: any[] = [];

  beforeEach(() => {
    queries = [];
    statements = [];
    mockDb = {
      withExclusiveTransactionAsync: jest.fn(async (cb) => {
        await cb();
      }),
      runAsync: jest.fn(async (query, params) => {
        queries.push({ query, params });
      }),
      prepareAsync: jest.fn(async (query) => {
        const stmt = {
          executeAsync: jest.fn(async (params) => {
            statements.push({ query, params });
          }),
          finalizeAsync: jest.fn(async () => {})
        };
        return stmt;
      }),
      getFirstAsync: jest.fn(async (query, params) => {
        return {
          id: params[0],
          principal_paise: queries[0].params[2],
          markup_factor: queries[0].params[3]
        };
      }),
      getAllAsync: jest.fn()
    };
    
    repo = new LoanRepository(mockDb);
  });

  it('Form-to-database loan creation integration test (â‚¹24,000, 30%, 12 months, Upfront Markup)', async () => {
    const loan = await repo.createLoanWithSchedule('test-borrower-id', {
      principal: toPaise(24000),
      markupFactor: 0.3,
      termMonths: 12,
      startDate: '2026-01-01',
      dueDay: 1,
      productType: 'instalment'
    });

    // Check loan creation query
    expect(queries.length).toBe(1);
    expect(queries[0].params[2]).toBe(toPaise(24000)); // principal
    expect(queries[0].params[3]).toBe(0.3); // markupFactor

    // Check schedules created
    expect(statements.length).toBe(12); // 12 months

    // Validate the first and last schedule
    const firstSchedule = statements[0].params;
    // index 5 = principal_component, index 6 = markup_component, index 7 = total_due
    expect(firstSchedule[5]).toBe(200000); // â‚¹2,000 principal per month
    expect(firstSchedule[6]).toBe(60000); // â‚¹600 markup per month
    expect(firstSchedule[7]).toBe(260000); // â‚¹2,600 total per month

    const lastSchedule = statements[11].params;
    expect(lastSchedule[5]).toBe(200000); // â‚¹2,000 principal
    expect(lastSchedule[6]).toBe(60000); // â‚¹600 markup
    expect(lastSchedule[7]).toBe(260000); // â‚¹2,600 total
  });
});

import { allocatePayment } from './allocation';
import { toPaise } from './money';

describe('Payment Allocation Engine (INSTALMENT_FIRST)', () => {
  it('allocates fully to oldest unpaid instalment', () => {
    const kists = [
      { kistNumber: 1, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0) },
      { kistNumber: 2, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0) }
    ];
    
    const result = allocatePayment({
      paymentAmount: toPaise(2600),
      kists,
      outstandingPenalty: toPaise(0)
    });

    expect(result.allocations.length).toBe(1);
    expect(result.allocations[0].allocatedToKist).toBe(1);
    expect(result.allocations[0].amount).toBe(toPaise(2600));
    expect(result.updatedKists[0].amountPaid).toBe(toPaise(2600));
    expect(result.updatedKists[0].status).toBe('PAID');
    expect(result.remainingPenalty).toBe(0);
  });

  it('allocates to instalment first, then penalty', () => {
    const kists = [
      { kistNumber: 1, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0) }
    ];
    
    // â‚¹2,600 instalment + â‚¹500 penalty. Pay â‚¹3,100.
    const result = allocatePayment({
      paymentAmount: toPaise(3100),
      kists,
      outstandingPenalty: toPaise(500)
    });

    expect(result.updatedKists[0].status).toBe('PAID');
    expect(result.remainingPenalty).toBe(0);
    const penaltyAllocation = result.allocations.find(a => a.allocatedToPenalty > 0);
    expect(penaltyAllocation?.allocatedToPenalty).toBe(toPaise(500));
  });

  it('marks instalment PAID and keeps penalty outstanding if payment exactly covers instalment', () => {
    const kists = [
      { kistNumber: 1, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0) }
    ];
    
    // â‚¹2,600 instalment + â‚¹500 penalty. Pay â‚¹2,600.
    const result = allocatePayment({
      paymentAmount: toPaise(2600),
      kists,
      outstandingPenalty: toPaise(500)
    });

    expect(result.updatedKists[0].status).toBe('PAID');
    expect(result.updatedKists[0].amountPaid).toBe(toPaise(2600));
    expect(result.remainingPenalty).toBe(toPaise(500));
    // penalty accrual stop is a logic in penalty engine, not allocation directly
  });

  it('allocates excess to next unpaid instalment (pre-payment)', () => {
    const kists = [
      { kistNumber: 1, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0) },
      { kistNumber: 2, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0) }
    ];
    
    // Pay â‚¹2,700 with 0 penalty
    const result = allocatePayment({
      paymentAmount: toPaise(2700),
      kists,
      outstandingPenalty: toPaise(0)
    });

    expect(result.updatedKists[0].status).toBe('PAID');
    expect(result.updatedKists[1].amountPaid).toBe(toPaise(100));
    expect(result.updatedKists[1].status).toBe('PARTIALLY_PAID');
    
    const secondAllocation = result.allocations.find(a => a.allocatedToKist === 2);
    expect(secondAllocation?.amount).toBe(toPaise(100));
  });

  it('cascades a large payment across multiple kists and penalties', () => {
    // Pay â‚¹6000. Penalty â‚¹500.
    // 1. Kist 1: â‚¹2,600 (remaining â‚¹3400)
    // 2. Penalty: â‚¹500 (remaining â‚¹2900)
    
    const kistsWithDueStatus = [
      { kistNumber: 1, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0), isDue: true },
      { kistNumber: 2, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0), isDue: true },
      { kistNumber: 3, amountDue: toPaise(2600), principalDue: toPaise(2000), markupDue: toPaise(600), amountPaid: toPaise(0), principalPaid: toPaise(0), markupPaid: toPaise(0), isDue: false }
    ];

    const result = allocatePayment({
      paymentAmount: toPaise(6000),
      kists: kistsWithDueStatus,
      outstandingPenalty: toPaise(500)
    });

    // Pay K1 (2600) -> Pay K2 (2600) -> Pay Penalty (500) -> Pay K3 (300)
    expect(result.updatedKists[0].status).toBe('PAID');
    expect(result.updatedKists[1].status).toBe('PAID');
    expect(result.remainingPenalty).toBe(0);
    expect(result.updatedKists[2].amountPaid).toBe(toPaise(300));
  });
});

import React from 'react';
import { View, Text } from 'react-native';
import LoansScreen from '../app/(tabs)/loans';

// A simple regression test to ensure that LoansScreen does not throw ReferenceError 
// or any other initialization error on import and render.

// Mock dependencies
jest.mock('expo-router', () => ({
  useRouter: () => ({ push: jest.fn() }),
}));

jest.mock('../core/database/DatabaseProvider', () => ({
  useDatabase: () => ({
    loans: { getAll: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue([]) },
    borrowers: { getAll: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue([]) }
  }),
}));

describe('LoansScreen', () => {
  it('imports and initializes without ReferenceError', () => {
    expect(LoansScreen).toBeDefined();
  });
});

module.exports = {
  preset: "jest-expo",
  transformIgnorePatterns: [
    "node_modules/(?!((jest-)?react-native|@react-native(-community)?)|expo(nent)?|@expo(nent)?/.*|@expo-google-fonts/.*|react-navigation|@react-navigation/.*|@sentry/react-native|native-base|react-native-svg)"
  ]
};
